The important thing: in CPython 3.8+, compiling an AST containing `ast.Name(\"False\", Load())` fails or asserts. In release builds it raises ValueError; in debug builds it asserts (abort). Let me check whether that's a bug... `\"False\" == \"True\"` is `False`, `False or None` is `None`. So `Expression.compile(\"False\")` would give None constant, which when evaluated returns None instead of False. But evaluate() is annotated `-> bool` and used as matcher result. The matcher's semantics: an identifier evaluates to whether the matcher matches it. If the identifier is literally \"False\", semantically it's a name \"False\" that should be looked up... In old behavior (pre-3.8), `compile(\"False\")` would produce code that does a LOAD_NAME \"False\", which with `__builtins__: {}` raises NameError at evaluation.\n\nHmm wait, actually what's the *correct* semantic? For pytest, `-k \"False\"` — the identifier \"False\" would be passed to the matcher function. So `evaluate(matcher)` with matcher returning True would return... in the old code, the compiled code does `LOAD_NAME \"False\"` then... with `{\"__builtins__\": {}}` globals and MatcherAdapter as locals. LOAD_NAME checks locals first — MatcherAdapter is a Mapping, and `__getitem__(\"False\")` returns matcher(\"False\"). So \"False\" would resolve through the matcher! So `-k \"False\"` means \"match tests whose name contains 'False'\".\n\nSo the pre-3.8 behavior: identifiers are always matcher lookups, including \"True\"/\"False\"/\"None\". The crash on 3.8+ happens because CPython no longer allows compiling AST names that are literally \"None\"/\"True\"/\"False\" in debug builds, and in release builds it now raises ValueError instead of compiling.\n\nSo what's the correct fix? The pytest fix decided that \"True\", \"False\", \"None\" in expressions should be treated as constants (Python literals) rather than matcher lookups. Let me verify with current pytest behavior: `Expression.compile(\"True\").evaluate(matcher)` returns True regardless of matcher.
